## Key Highlights

### 🔬 Research & Clinical Updates

1. **Tenecteplase vs Alteplase** - JAMA Neurology published data showing TNK superior for door-to-needle times (47 vs 52.7 min) and door-in-door-out times (108 vs 114 min) across 133,228 stroke patients

2. **Carotid Stenting with EVT** - Class II evidence supporting emergent carotid stenting during endovascular thrombectomy for tandem lesions improves 90-day outcomes

3. **Stroke Risk Factors** - Studies on alcohol intake, Brain Care Score (BCS), and statins for secondary prevention

### 💡 Clinical Pearls

- Dr. Sudhir Kumar warns about "normal" early CT scans in acute neurological deficits - MRI is often needed
- Imaging is redefining stroke care: "tissue is brain" vs traditional "time is brain" approach

### 🏥 Conference Announcements

- **13th International Conference on Stroke and Neurological Disorders** - September 28-29, 2026, Amsterdam, Netherlands

- **Text:** Answer: This is a paradoxical embolism.  
  
A deep vein thrombosis (DVT) crossed a right-to-left intracardiac shunt, most commonly a patent foramen ovale (PFO), bypassing the lungs and entering the systemic circulation, where it embolized to the brain and caused an ischemic stroke.

### esanum (@esanum)
- **Date:** 2026-07-20T14:44:06.000Z
- **URL:** https://x.com/esanum/status/2079215779912835117
- **Replies:** 0 | **Reposts:** 0 | **Likes:** 3 | **Views:** 204
- **Text:** Rapid BP reduction can worsen acute stroke  
 Chronic hypertension shifts cerebral autoregulation upward  
 Sudden normalization may reduce perfusion to the ischemic penumbra  
 This can enlarge the infarct and worsen deficits

This is a paradoxical embolism.

- **Text:** Day 19: Infective Endocarditis ---> Septic Embolic Stroke  
  
Infective endocarditis doesn't just destroy heart valves. Fragments of infected vegetations can break away, travel through the bloodstream, and lodge in the cerebral circulation, causing an ischemic stroke.  
  
The infected  
Medicine has patterns.  
  
Some diagnoses should immediately make you think of another.  
  
Starting today: one high-yield disease association every day.  
  
Follow the series.

- **Text:** Answer: This is a paradoxical embolism.

- **Text:** **Non-contrast CT head (NCCT).**  
  
For a stroke patient presenting ~3 hours after onset:  
- Immediate NCCT rules out hemorrhage (critical before tPA).  
- Quick, widely available, no contrast needed first.  
- Guides thrombolysis eligibility (window up to 4.5h) and next steps like CTA
